```text
Copy the FIRST attached reference photo EXACTLY: same camera position, same top-down-ish angle, same distance, same vertical 9:16 framing, same table surface at the same height and same angle, same two hands resting on the box in the same position and same grip, same arms entering frame the same way. Keep OUR product box exactly as it appears in the FIRST reference and in the SECOND attached reference: a tall upright rectangular tuck-top carton, square footprint, roughly 15 x 15 x 24 cm, forest green body, dark red band across the top with a gold THEXMASGLOW wordmark, cream serif headline CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR, a gold star-and-tree burst over a printed render of a smooth white dome-topped cylindrical projector on a separate white four-legged splayed stand base, slim red remote printed beside it, gold village skyline along the bottom, no transparent window. Keep the box at the same position, scale and angle it occupies in the FIRST reference. Change only three things.

FIRST - THE HANDS. A woman's hands, about 35, with warm olive skin and slender fingers, slender fingers, narrow wrists, smooth bare skin, short clean natural nails with no polish, no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch - the cuffs of a dusty-rose knitted wool sweater pushed back at both wrists. Same position on the box, same grip, same arms entering the frame the same way, same scale.

SECOND - THE TABLE. The surface under the box is now a pale cream terrazzo dining table with small terracotta and warm-white stone chips, a satin-matte surface and a square edge. Same height, same angle, same amount of the surface visible as in the FIRST reference.

THIRD - THE BACKGROUND. Behind and above the table, a festive Arts-and-Crafts dining room with muted ochre walls, dark cherry built-in shelves, a copper-framed mirror, a tiled fireplace with cream stockings and a tall Christmas tree with copper bells, red ribbons and warm white fairy lights. Every background detail stays in focus; the box remains dominant through its scale and placement.

Dress the dining table more festively: a sprig of fir and a few white berries, two or three small antique gold and pearl-white baubles, a short cream candle, a soft warm string light trailing across the matte table surface, all at the edges of frame so the box stays the clear subject.

Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, extremely high quality, tack-sharp focus on the box and the hands, crisp legible detail in the printing on the carton, perfect natural realistic lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

The wordmark printed on the red band must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere on the carton.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image other than the printing on our own carton.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact layout, product geometry, hand positions and camera. @Image 2 owns the established photographic look and carton artwork only; use the new hands, materials and background specified above. All parts of this photograph, including the background, are in crisp natural iPhone deep focus, no portrait mode, no bokeh, no blur.
```

```text

AUDIO RULE - HIGHEST PRIORITY, READ THIS FIRST: this clip has NO MUSIC OF ANY KIND. No background song, no soundtrack, no score, no melody, no instruments, no singing, no humming, no jingle, no Christmas song, no festive music bed, no ambient musical pad, no strings, no piano, no bells used as music. The audio track contains ONLY real natural sound recorded in that room. No video reference is attached. If any music appears anywhere in the 4 seconds the clip is wrong.

HAND ROLES - SECOND MOST IMPORTANT: THE RIGHT HAND opens the lid and then grips and lifts the projector alone. THE LEFT HAND stays braced against the lower left side of THE BOX from first frame to last, fingers resting below the main printed artwork. The left hand never lifts, follows the projector, gestures, reaches into the box or leaves the carton. It steadies the box on the table while the right hand and product rise. Exactly two hands with separate jobs: right hand carries the product, left hand anchors the carton.

EXIT RULE - THIRD MOST IMPORTANT: the projector does NOT stay in frame at the end. THE RIGHT HAND lifts it out and keeps going straight up until the projector and right hand leave the top of frame. At 4.0s the open carton remains on the table, held steady by THE LEFT HAND. The right hand never pauses to present the product.

SPEED RULE - FOURTH MOST IMPORTANT: the hands work at a brisk, confident, real-time pace. This is a normal person opening a box at normal speed, not a careful slow-motion demonstration. The lift is QUICK and decisive. Do not slow anything down.

TASK: image-to-video. The start image is frame 0.0s. Hold its table, box artwork, props and lighting exactly.

[1] SUMMARY
First-person POV: two hands open a tall dark-green Christmas carton on a pale cream terrazzo dining table with small terracotta and warm-white stone chips, a satin-matte surface and a square edge, lift the white projector briskly straight up out of it and carry it out of the top of frame. Locked POV looking down. 4 seconds, one take.

[2] GLOBAL STYLE
Real handheld phone POV, warm domestic Christmas light, crisp and sharp, correctly exposed. Very slight handheld drift. Real-time speed throughout. No cinematic grade, no grain, no slow motion, no speed ramp.

[3] ASSET ROLES
The start image AND @Image 1 are the same approved image, attached as start_image and image reference. They alone control frame 0.0s, the exact printed box, hands, table, room, props and light. Begin on that exact view and animate it. No reference video, no alternate carton, no plain-box substitute.

[4] OBJECTS
THE BOX - a dark-green tuck-top carton standing upright, front panel facing camera, a dark red band across the top with a gold THEXMASGLOW wordmark, the cream serif words CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR, a gold star-and-tree burst over a printed white dome-topped projector on a white four-legged splayed stand, a gold village skyline along the bottom, no transparent window. About 15 x 15 x 24 cm. Plain interior, no insert, no foam tray.
THE PROJECTOR - inside THE BOX: a matte-white dome-shaped body on a separate white four-legged splayed stand. Set into the top of the dome is a flat circular BLACK perforated mesh disc. The top face is BLACK and stays black for every frame. No front lens, no camera head, no swivel.
THE HANDS - exactly two hands, a woman's, with the cuff of a dusty-rose knitted wool sweater pushed back at each wrist and no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch.

[5] LOCATION - a pale cream terrazzo dining table with small terracotta and warm-white stone chips, a satin-matte surface and a square edge fills the frame. The lit cream candle, fir sprigs, white berries, gold and pearl-white baubles and warm string lights stay exactly where they are in the start image. Behind them, sharply in focus, a festive Arts-and-Crafts dining room with muted ochre walls, dark cherry built-in shelves, a copper-framed mirror, a tiled fireplace with cream stockings and a tall Christmas tree with copper bells, red ribbons and warm white fairy lights. The same table, carton, decorations and background remain visible through the final frame.

[6] FIRST FRAME - exactly the supplied image: THE BOX closed and upright with its full printed front facing the camera; the right hand on the lid and the left hand bracing the lower side in their exact shown positions.

[7] TIMELINE - brisk real-time pacing, every beat lands on time
0.0-0.7s - camera locked. THE LEFT HAND remains braced on the lower left side of THE BOX. THE RIGHT HAND slides its fingers under the front top flap and lifts it sharply; the tuck tongue pops out of the front panel.
0.7-1.4s - the lid flaps fold upward and backward away from camera on the rear fold in one quick motion, staying attached to THE BOX; the dark interior comes into view.
1.4-2.1s - THE RIGHT HAND reaches into THE BOX and grips the upper side of THE PROJECTOR firmly with thumb and fingers, leaving the BLACK perforated mesh disc visible. THE LEFT HAND remains on the outside of THE BOX.
2.1-3.0s - THE RIGHT HAND lifts THE PROJECTOR QUICKLY and decisively straight up and out of THE BOX in one brisk continuous vertical pull, clearing the rim in well under a second. The BLACK top face is clearly visible as it rises. THE LEFT HAND remains planted on THE BOX.
3.0-3.6s - THE RIGHT HAND DOES NOT STOP. It keeps rising in the same continuous motion and carries THE PROJECTOR completely out of the top of frame. THE LEFT HAND stays on THE BOX at the same low height.
3.6-4.0s - THE PROJECTOR and THE RIGHT HAND are out of frame. THE LEFT HAND remains resting against the lower side of the open empty carton on the pale cream terrazzo dining table with small terracotta and warm-white stone chips, a satin-matte surface and a square edge, lid flaps folded back. The printed front artwork, candle, fir, berries, fireplace and tree remain visible. Nothing else moves.

[9] CAMERA AND OPTICS - one static POV looking down at the table at roughly 50 degrees, vertical 9:16, normal wide-angle phone lens, very slight handheld drift only. The camera NEVER follows the product upward - it stays exactly where it is and lets the product exit the frame. No push-in, no orbit, no zoom, no tilt, no cut.

[10] LIGHTING - warm indoor candle and fairy-light mix, unchanged for the whole clip.

[11] PHYSICS - cardboard behaves like cardboard: the flaps crease on their folds and stay attached to THE BOX. THE BOX stays flat on the table and does not slide, tip or lift. The box rim rocks very slightly as the product is pulled out quickly, and settles against the left hand, which remains on the carton.

[12] AUDIO - DRY DOCUMENTARY AUDIO ONLY. The track contains exactly two things and nothing else: cardboard flaps creasing and fingers moving on the carton. Total silence apart from those. NO MUSIC, NO SONG, NO SCORE, NO SOUNDTRACK, NO INSTRUMENTS, NO VOICE.

[13] POSITIVE LOCKS - the lid is never lifted off, never removed, never carried aside, never set down. THE BOX never leaves the table. Exactly two hands: the right lifts the product; the left remains on the box throughout. The top face of THE PROJECTOR is BLACK. The lift out of the box is fast. THE PROJECTOR exits the top of frame and the last frame shows the open printed box on the table with the left hand still bracing it.
BANNED: any music, song, score or soundtrack; the left hand rising with the projector, leaving the box, waving, hovering or disappearing; both hands lifting the product; the right hand pausing to present the product; the product still in frame at 4.0s; changing the supplied first frame, replacing the printed box with a plain green box, erasing or redesigning the wordmark, product illustration or lettering, turning the printed front away; the camera following the product; slow motion; an all-white product top, a front lens, a swivel head, a third hand; disappearing decorations; a second box; a cut; subtitles; added overlay text.
```

### Image prompt
```text
COPY @Image 1 EXACTLY. The ONLY things that change are the hands, the countertop material and the kitchen behind them. Everything else is a literal copy.

KEEP IDENTICAL TO @Image 1: the camera position, the camera height, the lens, the distance, the vertical 9:16 crop, the exact pose of both hands, the exact grip on the product, the product's size and position and tilt in frame, where each forearm enters the frame, and the countertop.

THE COUNTERTOP MUST BE IN FRAME. Exactly as in @Image 1, the countertop occupies the LOWER RIGHT of the frame as a clean wedge running diagonally, passing behind and below the right forearm, clearly visible right up to the bottom edge of the picture. DO NOT remove the countertop. DO NOT replace the lower part of the frame with floor. NO floor is visible anywhere in this shot.

THE HANDS - this is the first change. Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home at Christmas.

TASK: image-to-video, one continuous handheld phone POV take, vertical 9:16. Frame 0.0s is the start image: a natural dark-walnut six-panel solid wood door with two small square upper panels and four taller panels beneath, a satin wax finish and clearly visible walnut grain fully closed with a wreath, a hand on the brass knob. @Video 1 is a room-content reference for the layout, coved ceiling, furniture, the children's identities and clothes, colours, and moving projection artwork. Use the camera and child actions specified below, not the reference video's camera or its specific child actions. Only the closed-door start image defines frame 0.0s. No end frame is supplied.

THE SIX MOVING THINGS - all six running from the first frame to the last.

SMOKE. Inside the flat projected North Pole log-cabin gift depot picture, illustrated smoke curls rise from the depicted tiny chimneys, leaning right, widening and fading while fresh drawn curls form below. These curls are moving pixels lying flush on the plaster or stone. The real fireplace, mantel and chimney breast release no smoke, sparks, objects or animation into the room.

THE RIBBONS. The crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbons RADIATE OUTWARD FROM THE GREAT STAR and ripple as they go, like long silk curtains hung in a draught - a slow swell travels down the length of each band away from the star, brightening it as it passes and combing its lower fringe into new folds. The fan stays where it is on the plaster; the swell inside it travels.

THE SLEIGH. Santa's sleigh and its eight reindeer are FLYING ACROSS THE CEILING the way a plane crosses the sky, arcing in toward the great star on a long sweeping curve. Legs gallop, heads nod, the sleigh rocks, the long trail of light streams off the runners, the team rises and dips. They start near one side of the ceiling and finish far across it, closer to the star than they began. Never parked.

THE WOODLAND DEER AND THE ELVES. The illustrated deer travel steadily among the projected fir trees along the lower wall, legs walking and heads nodding, moving about one body length every second. Existing rabbits make small grounded hops within the wall drawing; elves bend, lift existing wrapped gifts and pass them toward the red sleigh at the log-cabin depot. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.

THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.

THE SNOW. The big soft six-point flakes and the tiny points of light fall the way heavy snow falls past a window at night - down and slightly sideways, each turning on its own centre. One fades out at the floor line as a fresh one fades in high near the ceiling, so the density never thins.

Everything else travels too: the existing tiny elves keep carrying and passing wrapped gifts beside the log-cabin gift depot, shooting stars streak and fade, the flat projected bauble and candy-cane drawings rotate and travel along the wall and ceiling surfaces, the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with their clappers swinging inside them, and the lit log-cabin gift depot windows flicker warm. Only the great gold Christmas star holds its centre position; its light simply breathes brighter and dimmer.

ONE PROJECTOR, ONE PICTURE: whatever the wall is doing, the ceiling is doing at the same instant and the same speed. The picture's outer boundary is anchored - it never rotates, spins or slides across the room as one sheet; only the things inside it travel. It is flat light lying on the actual room surfaces. The broad flat central ceiling and all four smooth coved transitions remain visible. The sleigh trail and garlands run across the flat panel then curve and progressively stretch around the rounded coves before continuing vertically down the walls; no invented ridge or angular fold interrupts the coves. At the chimney breast the picture breaks sharply around the two projecting vertical corners, carrying across every brick face with crisp mortar-line texture and tiny mortar shadows. The arched doorway and window reveals interrupt the light at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. Nothing ever leaves a surface, and nothing is projected on the floor, rug, sofa, table, tree or gifts.

THE CEILING IS FULL. The ceiling carries as much moving picture as the walls do, everywhere it appears in frame and above all in the closing seconds: the great star, the travelling gold light waves, the rippling red-and-gold ribbons, the flying sleigh, pouring snow and twinkling stars run across the whole coved ceiling and over every ceiling seam, right into all four corners. No bare un-animated patch of plaster and no bare ceiling detail anywhere in the closing framing.

0.0-0.6s - The gentle upward tilt and gentle forward drift have ALREADY STARTED on the first frame and continue at constant speed while the hand turns the knob and pushes the door away from camera in one quick decisive shove. The room behind it is already running AND BOTH CHILDREN ARE ALREADY IN MOTION - the widening gap only uncovers more of a movie that was already playing and two kids who were already running before the door opened. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain painted wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap.
0.6s to the end - CONTINUE THE SAME SIMULTANEOUS FORWARD-AND-UPWARD MOVE ALREADY IN PROGRESS. Clearing the door is not a camera cue. All through it the smoke pours from every chimney, the ribbons ripples, the sleigh flies on across the ceiling, the deer procession and the elves keep moving, the travelling gold light waves keep spreading and the snow keeps falling.
Inside that single move, the children are playing ONE CONTINUOUS GAME OF CHASE on the open floor. They are already mid-stride before the door reveals them. The girl with East Asian features, light warm skin and long straight dark hair  runs a broad curving path across the rug and alongside the sofa; the boy with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part follows two steps behind, both laughing, taking quick alternating footfalls with swinging arms. They continue their running loop naturally around the available floor space, curving away from furniture and each other. Their positions change in every successive instant, including the middle of the shot. They do not arrive at a destination, stop beside each other, face the camera, line up, switch activities or climb onto the sofa. This is already-running, still-running, keep-running: the same continuous playful action before the door opens, through the reveal and through the last frame in which they are visible. They may glance up while their feet keep stepping, but their momentum never stops. Real speed, grounded feet and natural weight; no sliding, teleporting or running in place.

CAMERA - eye height, normal wide-angle phone lens, subtle natural handheld sway. One blended forward-and-upward move, with constant gentle upward angular speed across all five seconds. No delayed tilt, no pitch jump, no sudden reframe, no acceleration or speed ramp, no stop, no second move, no cut, no zoom, no orbit, no fisheye, no lens warping. POSITIVE LOCKS - real-time motion at normal speed. One continuous take. ROOM AND IDENTITY - a spacious double-height Arts-and-Crafts Christmas great room with a high COVED CEILING: a broad perfectly flat rectangular central ceiling panel, connected to all four vertical walls by deep smoothly curved plaster coves, with no ridge, no beam, no barrel tunnel and no coffer grid. A floor-to-ceiling brick chimney breast in small red-brown bricks stands on the back wall with a dark cherry mantel; a wide three-part rectangular window occupies the left wall and a rounded archway opens on the right. Medium-brown cherry floorboards, a large oatmeal linen sectional sofa, a rectangular upholstered ottoman and a cream wool rug. The children are a boy of about five with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part and a girl of about eight with East Asian features, light warm skin and long straight dark hair with a red velvet hairband, wearing warm red and ivory knitted Christmas jumpers with gold bells and green fir-tree patterns, navy knitted lounge trousers and cream wool socks. Preserve these identities and this architecture from @Video 1.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical WOODLAND CHRISTMAS GIFT NIGHT: a snowy forest of enormous decorated fir trees, every branch hung with crimson baubles, gold candle lights and tiny ribboned gifts; nestled between the trees, warmly lit log cottages decorated with holly wreaths and red bows; friendly illustrated deer with gold bells and rabbits beside little stacks of wrapped Christmas presents. A red sleigh filled with colourful ribboned parcels waits outside Santa's warm log-cabin gift depot, with red-coated elves carrying teddy bears and wooden rocking horses toward it. Two monumental gold Christmas lanterns flank a towering fully decorated tree; every ornament has a crisp rim and every gift ribbon a visible knot. Along the upper walls, paired gold bells, holly clusters and richly woven evergreen swags. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa and eight reindeer sweep past a gold Christmas star, with curling red-and-gold ribbon trails interwoven with holly leaves, candy canes, delicate cream snowflakes and tiny star lights. This is unmistakably Christmas Eve, red and gold dominant with deep fir-green accents, warm windows and a dark midnight background. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

PRIMARY REQUIREMENT: this is a continuously ANIMATED Christmas movie projected on real walls and ceiling. The sleigh travels, reindeer gallop and smoke actively billows from every illustrated house chimney. Most other existing illustrated subjects move too. A static decorative image behind moving children is a failed result. Apply the detailed PROJECTED FILM ACTION below at every instant; retain the existing camera and child choreography.

CAMERA CONTINUITY - PRIMARY CORRECTION: one simultaneous forward-and-upward movement spans the entire 5-second take. Beginning immediately at 0.0s, move forward gently while tilting upward gently at the SAME TIME. Keep the upward angular speed constant from first frame to last: approximately 5 degrees per second, about 25 degrees total. At 1s the upward tilt is already 5 degrees, at 2s 10 degrees, at 3s 15 degrees, at 4s 20 degrees, at 5s 25 degrees. These are points on ONE continuous movement, not separate beats. There is no forward-only opening, level hold, late tilt onset, sudden upward swing, acceleration, whip, or change of direction. The hand opens the door during this same gentle rising move. Once the threshold is clear, continue walking forward and rising at that exact same unhurried rate. The last frame still includes the back wall and some furniture under the ceiling; do not rush toward a ceiling-only final composition. @Video 1 supplies visual content ONLY: its delayed upward camera tilt is specifically WRONG for this shot and must not be copied.

CHILD MOVEMENT - PRIMARY CORRECTION: Behind the door two children are already playing chase, and they KEEP RUNNING throughout the shot. Show one continuous flow of alternating footfalls, changing positions and swinging arms. There is no arrival, regrouping, pause, pose or second activity. This movement has priority over matching any reference composition. The same closed-door opening and forward walk with upward tilt follow below.

HIGHEST PRIORITY, READ THIS FIRST: the pictures on the walls and the coved ceiling are A MOVIE THAT IS PLAYING, not a pattern painted on the plaster. @Video 1 supplies the room design, children's identities and moving projection artwork only. It is a visual room reference, not a timeline or camera reference. No frame of it is a target frame for this clip. Behind the closed door the projected movie is already playing, and it keeps running through the last frame. Smoke pours from every chimney, the ribbons radiates, the sleigh flies, the deer procession travels and the elves work, the travelling gold light waves spread, the snow falls - continuously, in every single frame. If the shapes on the walls and ceiling hold still, the clip has failed.

Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home at Christmas.

TASK: image-to-video, one continuous handheld phone POV take, vertical 9:16. Frame 0.0s is the start image: a natural dark-walnut six-panel solid wood door with two small square upper panels and four taller panels beneath, a satin wax finish and clearly visible walnut grain fully closed with a wreath, a hand on the brass knob. @Video 1 is a room-content reference for the layout, coved ceiling, furniture, the children's identities and clothes, colours, and moving projection artwork. Use the camera and child actions specified below, not the reference video's camera or its specific child actions. Only the closed-door start image defines frame 0.0s. No end frame is supplied.

PROJECTED FILM ACTION - THE MAIN SUBJECT IS THE MOVING CHRISTMAS ARTWORK. Animate the existing illustrated objects themselves, not just the camera, children, brightness or glitter. At every moment MOST visible illustrated subjects are visibly changing position or shape independently. Keep the physical room and the projection's overall footprint fixed.
SLEIGH AND REINDEER: the existing sleigh team travels along the plaster ceiling at roughly one fifth of the visible ceiling width per second, crossing architectural seams and passing the fixed central star. Every reindeer continuously cycles its legs in a running gallop; heads bob, harnesses flex and the sleigh rocks behind them. The team must move relative to the stationary beams, star and ceiling corners in every half-second interval. Do not rotate the ceiling picture as a single disc.
CHIMNEYS: each visible illustrated house chimney continuously emits a clearly visible cream-grey animated smoke plume, contained INSIDE the flat wall illustration. New curls emerge every 0.25 second, rise one roof-height per second, expand and deform, then dissolve as more curls follow. Several different plumes must be visibly evolving at once; none may remain a frozen drawn squiggle. These are moving pixels on the wall, never real smoke entering the room.
VILLAGE: the existing illustrated figures walk along the wall, take full alternating steps and swing arms. Gift carriers lift and pass the existing parcels. Existing animal drawings walk with articulating legs, toy processions advance and wheels turn where present. Existing house-window light flickers independently. Buildings stay fixed so the moving figures and chimney smoke read clearly.
RIBBONS, GARLANDS AND BELLS: successive waves visibly deform each existing ribbon along its length while bright gold crests travel from the central star toward the walls, covering half a ribbon length per second. Existing bell drawings swing left-right-left roughly once per second with independently swinging clappers. Existing ornament drawings rotate within the projected picture. Do not leave the wall decorations frozen while only the ceiling sleigh moves.
SNOW AND STARS: existing snowflake drawings translate along the ceiling planes and down the walls continuously, at different speeds. Ceiling snow stays flush to ceiling plaster and follows its slope, never drops into the air. Stars twinkle independently while existing shooting-star streaks traverse the surface. Nothing moves over the boy or children, furniture, floor or in the open room.
All these actions run simultaneously from the first visible projected frame until the final frame, at normal playback speed, without a fade-in, opening pause, final freeze or settling back into the reference image. The supplied visual references fix architecture, identities and artwork appearance ONLY; replace their static or weak artwork motion with the explicit motion above. The original full projection is already on at frame zero or behind the closed door. All animated pixels bend at actual wall and ceiling seams, remain flat on those surfaces and are occluded by real foreground objects.

ONE PROJECTOR, ONE PICTURE: whatever the wall is doing, the ceiling is doing at the same instant and the same speed. The picture's outer boundary is anchored - it never rotates, spins or slides across the room as one sheet; only the things inside it travel. It is flat light lying on the actual room surfaces. The broad flat central ceiling and all four smooth coved transitions remain visible. The sleigh trail and garlands run across the flat panel then curve and progressively stretch around the rounded coves before continuing vertically down the walls; no invented ridge or angular fold interrupts the coves. At the chimney breast the picture breaks sharply around the two projecting vertical corners, carrying across every brick face with crisp mortar-line texture and tiny mortar shadows. The arched doorway and window reveals interrupt the light at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. Nothing ever leaves a surface, and nothing is projected on the floor, rug, sofa, table, tree or gifts.

THE CEILING IS FULL. The ceiling carries as much moving picture as the walls do, everywhere it appears in frame and above all in the closing seconds: the great star, the travelling gold light waves, the rippling red-and-gold ribbons, the flying sleigh, pouring snow and twinkling stars run across the whole coved ceiling and over every ceiling seam, right into all four corners. No bare un-animated patch of plaster and no bare ceiling detail anywhere in the closing framing.

BEATS
0.0-0.6s - The gentle upward tilt and gentle forward drift have ALREADY STARTED on the first frame and continue at constant speed while the hand turns the knob and pushes the door away from camera in one quick decisive shove. The room behind it is already running AND BOTH CHILDREN ARE ALREADY IN MOTION - the widening gap only uncovers more of a movie that was already playing and two kids who were already running before the door opened. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain painted wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap.
0.6s to the end - CONTINUE THE SAME SIMULTANEOUS FORWARD-AND-UPWARD MOVE ALREADY IN PROGRESS. Clearing the door is not a camera cue. The view must not travel straight ahead first and only later rise to the ceiling. The camera advances slowly through the doorway while its aim keeps rising at the same small constant angular rate that began at 0.0s; room features slide downward through the frame continuously as perspective gently advances. No hold on the children, no stop at the sofa, no separate ceiling reveal. Do not speed up the tilt in the last two seconds to finish a composition. The shot simply ends during the same ongoing gentle movement, with both back wall and ceiling still visible. All through it the smoke pours from every chimney, the ribbons ripples, the sleigh flies on across the ceiling, the deer procession and the elves keep moving, the travelling gold light waves keep spreading and the snow keeps falling.
Inside that single move, the children are playing ONE CONTINUOUS GAME OF CHASE on the open floor. They are already mid-stride before the door reveals them. The girl with East Asian features, light warm skin and long straight dark hair  runs a broad curving path across the rug and alongside the sofa; the boy with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part follows two steps behind, both laughing, taking quick alternating footfalls with swinging arms. They continue their running loop naturally around the available floor space, curving away from furniture and each other. Their positions change in every successive instant, including the middle of the shot. They do not arrive at a destination, stop beside each other, face the camera, line up, switch activities or climb onto the sofa. This is already-running, still-running, keep-running: the same continuous playful action before the door opens, through the reveal and through the last frame in which they are visible. They may glance up while their feet keep stepping, but their momentum never stops. Real speed, grounded feet and natural weight; no sliding, teleporting or running in place.

CAMERA - eye height, normal wide-angle phone lens, subtle natural handheld sway. One blended forward-and-upward move, with constant gentle upward angular speed across all five seconds. No delayed tilt, no pitch jump, no sudden reframe, no acceleration or speed ramp, no stop, no second move, no cut, no zoom, no orbit, no fisheye, no lens warping. The camera is the person's eyes; no phone, screen or device in frame after 0.6s.

EXACTLY TWO CHILDREN, no adults, no third child, no pets. Hold the sharpness and exposure of the start image - the panel edges of the door, the brass knob, the forearm and sleeve exactly as shown in the starting image, with no jewellery. Crisp phone footage, in focus first frame to last.

AUDIO - dry documentary sound only: the handle, the door swinging, continuous alternating running footfalls, children laughing, quiet room tone. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS - real-time motion at normal speed. One continuous take. One single smooth camera move with forward translation and upward tilt happening simultaneously from the first frame, at unchanged gentle speed throughout. The projected picture is visibly in motion in every single frame, at the start, in the middle and in the last second. The whole coved ceiling is covered in moving picture at the end. BOTH CHILDREN ARE MOVING IN EVERY FRAME THEY APPEAR IN, from the first frame the door uncovers them to the last frame of the clip: both children keep running their playful curving loop; the camera may tilt them out of view but never catches them settled into a pose. Their movement is natural, unchoreographed, real-speed kid movement. Sharp focus to the final frame.

BANNED - a still or frozen picture; a projection that appears to move only because the camera moves; light that moves for a moment then stops; chimneys without smoke; an ribbons that sits still; a parked sleigh; a deer procession that does not travel or elves that do not move; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that twinkle but never travel; a still ceiling above a moving wall; an empty or un-animated area of ceiling or an un-animated patch of the vault in any frame; the whole picture rotating or sliding as one sheet; a pattern that looks printed, painted or like wallpaper; a glow or bright seam along the door edge or gap; light that grows or fills the room as the door opens; particles falling through the open air of the room; volumetric snow; snow in front of the children, the sofa, the stair or the real tree; a camera that moves, stops and then moves again; two separate camera moves; a forward-only push followed by a late upward tilt; holding a level angle until entering the room; a sudden ceiling swing; accelerating the tilt near the end; copying @Video 1's camera timing; either child climbing or jumping onto the sofa; either child standing still, waiting, hesitating, posed or rooted to one spot at any point; a child who only turns her head while her feet stay planted; a child who begins the clip motionless and starts moving later; a child who stops moving before the last frame; the projector unit visible; slow motion; blur; any music; a cut; an adult; subtitles; on-screen text.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the deer procession advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

REFERENCE-ONLY LOCK - @Video 1 provides room, identity and projection appearance only. No static room image is attached. Preserve the closed-door starting frame and the established single walking-and-tilting camera move. The children are continuously running a playful chase; their actions are independent of all poses and activity changes in the reference video. Never copy the reference video's opening composition, sofa bouncing, camera movement, cuts or timing. The new clip is exactly 5 seconds and has no end frame.

SURFACE-ONLY LOCK - every animated Christmas shape, including every shape above the real fireplace, is flat projected light attached to the existing plaster, chimney surface and ceiling. Moving drawings follow the surface perspective and bend or clip at actual corners and ceiling details. They have no thickness, volume, free-space parallax or physical cast shadow. The real fireplace opening and mantel remain real stationary architecture. No character, sleigh, ribbon, spark, snowflake, smoke curl or ornament floats between the camera and a wall, emerges from the fireplace, hovers above the mantel, or enters the usable room. Apparent depth belongs only to the drawn illustration on the wall.

ROOM AND IDENTITY - a spacious double-height Arts-and-Crafts Christmas great room with a high COVED CEILING: a broad perfectly flat rectangular central ceiling panel, connected to all four vertical walls by deep smoothly curved plaster coves, with no ridge, no beam, no barrel tunnel and no coffer grid. A floor-to-ceiling brick chimney breast in small red-brown bricks stands on the back wall with a dark cherry mantel; a wide three-part rectangular window occupies the left wall and a rounded archway opens on the right. Medium-brown cherry floorboards, a large oatmeal linen sectional sofa, a rectangular upholstered ottoman and a cream wool rug. The children are a boy of about five with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part and a girl of about eight with East Asian features, light warm skin and long straight dark hair with a red velvet hairband, wearing warm red and ivory knitted Christmas jumpers with gold bells and green fir-tree patterns, navy knitted lounge trousers and cream wool socks. Preserve these identities and this architecture from @Video 1.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical WOODLAND CHRISTMAS GIFT NIGHT: a snowy forest of enormous decorated fir trees, every branch hung with crimson baubles, gold candle lights and tiny ribboned gifts; nestled between the trees, warmly lit log cottages decorated with holly wreaths and red bows; friendly illustrated deer with gold bells and rabbits beside little stacks of wrapped Christmas presents. A red sleigh filled with colourful ribboned parcels waits outside Santa's warm log-cabin gift depot, with red-coated elves carrying teddy bears and wooden rocking horses toward it. Two monumental gold Christmas lanterns flank a towering fully decorated tree; every ornament has a crisp rim and every gift ribbon a visible knot. Along the upper walls, paired gold bells, holly clusters and richly woven evergreen swags. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa and eight reindeer sweep past a gold Christmas star, with curling red-and-gold ribbon trails interwoven with holly leaves, candy canes, delicate cream snowflakes and tiny star lights. This is unmistakably Christmas Eve, red and gold dominant with deep fir-green accents, warm windows and a dark midnight background. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

The sleigh trail and garlands run across the flat panel then curve and progressively stretch around the rounded coves before continuing vertically down the walls; no invented ridge or angular fold interrupts the coves. Nothing ever leaves a surface, and nothing is projected on the floor, rug, sofa, table, tree or gifts.

THE CEILING IS FULL. The ceiling carries as much moving picture as the walls do, everywhere it appears in frame and above all in the closing seconds: the great star, the travelling gold light waves, the rippling red-and-gold ribbons, the flying sleigh, pouring snow and twinkling stars run across the whole coved ceiling and over every ceiling seam, right into all four corners. 0.0-0.6s - The gentle upward tilt and gentle forward drift have ALREADY STARTED on the first frame and continue at constant speed while the hand turns the knob and pushes the door away from camera in one quick decisive shove. The girl with East Asian features, light warm skin and long straight dark hair  runs a broad curving path across the rug and alongside the sofa; the boy with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part follows two steps behind, both laughing, taking quick alternating footfalls with swinging arms. ROOM AND IDENTITY - a spacious double-height Arts-and-Crafts Christmas great room with a high COVED CEILING: a broad perfectly flat rectangular central ceiling panel, connected to all four vertical walls by deep smoothly curved plaster coves, with no ridge, no beam, no barrel tunnel and no coffer grid. A floor-to-ceiling brick chimney breast in small red-brown bricks stands on the back wall with a dark cherry mantel; a wide three-part rectangular window occupies the left wall and a rounded archway opens laterally on the living room's right side wall, outside the entrance route; the starting walnut door itself opens directly into this room through one normal wall-thickness threshold. The children are a boy of about five with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part and a girl of about eight with East Asian features, light warm skin and long straight dark hair with a red velvet hairband, wearing warm red and ivory knitted Christmas jumpers with gold bells and green fir-tree patterns, navy knitted lounge trousers and cream wool socks. Preserve these identities and this architecture from @Video 1.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical WOODLAND CHRISTMAS GIFT NIGHT: a snowy forest of enormous decorated fir trees, every branch hung with crimson baubles, gold candle lights and tiny ribboned gifts; nestled between the trees, warmly lit log cottages decorated with holly wreaths and red bows; friendly illustrated deer with gold bells and rabbits beside little stacks of wrapped Christmas presents. A red sleigh filled with colourful ribboned parcels waits outside Santa's warm log-cabin gift depot, with red-coated elves carrying teddy bears and wooden rocking horses toward it. Two monumental gold Christmas lanterns flank a towering fully decorated tree; every ornament has a crisp rim and every gift ribbon a visible knot. Along the upper walls, paired gold bells, holly clusters and richly woven evergreen swags. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa and eight reindeer sweep past a gold Christmas star, with curling red-and-gold ribbon trails interwoven with holly leaves, candy canes, delicate cream snowflakes and tiny star lights. This is unmistakably Christmas Eve, red and gold dominant with deep fir-green accents, warm windows and a dark midnight background. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The two roof slopes, straight central ridge and three white-painted tie beams remain clearly legible. The sleigh and garlands fold at the ridge and stretch down both roof planes. Each tie beam catches its own slice of the projected picture on the face toward the source, with a clear offset to the plaster beyond and a narrow hard shadow behind it. At the triangular gable and window casings the picture breaks again along the actual wall-to-ceiling and trim edges. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The timber floorboards, the large flat-weave rug, the bed and its covers, the bench at its foot and the little Christmas tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. The boy's face and his knitted jumper catch that spill so his face reads clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no stool, no side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain wall and floor.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the knit stitches of the jumpers, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the plaster, the crisp architectural edges of the ceiling and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
```

Wholesome family Christmas scene, a child at home with his family at Christmas.

SUMMARY: a boy bounces on his bed in a Christmas bedroom at night while a projected Christmas village picture is PLAYING across the cream plaster wall behind the bed and the whole pitched ceiling with tie beams above it, thrown there by a projector standing on the floor. 5 seconds, one continuous handheld phone take, vertical.

GLOBAL STYLE: crisp real iPhone footage at night, handheld, real-time speed. Sharp and in focus from the first frame to the last, at least as sharp as the start image. No blur, no haze, no soft focus, no washed-out exposure, no colour shift.

ASSET ROLES: @Image 1 is frame 0.0s and controls a snug bedroom under a steep PITCHED CEILING with two smooth plaster slopes meeting at a straight central ridge, crossed by three slender white-painted horizontal tie beams with crisp square edges; the back wall is a simple triangular gable with two small side windows. A warm cherry wooden bed with a tall arched headboard is centred on that wall, red-and-cream checked bedding, cherry bedside chests, medium-brown timber floorboards and a cream wool rug. Preserve the exact furniture placement, child identities, clothing, room colours and framing in that supplied image.

SUBJECTS:
THE BOY - exactly one child, a boy of about five with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part, wearing warm red and ivory knitted Christmas jumpers with gold bells and green fir-tree patterns, navy knitted lounge trousers and cream wool socks, standing on the bed with his arms thrown wide. No second child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the cream plaster wall and the whole pitched ceiling with tie beams. Everything glowing on the plaster is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical WOODLAND CHRISTMAS GIFT NIGHT: a snowy forest of enormous decorated fir trees, every branch hung with crimson baubles, gold candle lights and tiny ribboned gifts; nestled between the trees, warmly lit log cottages decorated with holly wreaths and red bows; friendly illustrated deer with gold bells and rabbits beside little stacks of wrapped Christmas presents. A red sleigh filled with colourful ribboned parcels waits outside Santa's warm log-cabin gift depot, with red-coated elves carrying teddy bears and wooden rocking horses toward it. Two monumental gold Christmas lanterns flank a towering fully decorated tree; every ornament has a crisp rim and every gift ribbon a visible knot. Along the upper walls, paired gold bells, holly clusters and richly woven evergreen swags. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa and eight reindeer sweep past a gold Christmas star, with curling red-and-gold ribbon trails interwoven with holly leaves, candy canes, delicate cream snowflakes and tiny star lights. This is unmistakably Christmas Eve, red and gold dominant with deep fir-green accents, warm windows and a dark midnight background. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

HOW THE PICTURE BEHAVES - THIS IS THE ENTIRE POINT OF THE CLIP. In every previous attempt the picture sat dead still like printed wallpaper and the clip was worthless. THE PICTURE IS A FILM PLAYING ON A CINEMA SCREEN: the screen is the plaster and holds perfectly still, while everything inside the picture is in constant motion for all five seconds. The supplied starting frame already has the complete projection at full brightness. Continue motion inside that existing picture immediately; no fade-in or gradual appearance. Matching @Image 1 applies to the bedroom, the bed, THE BOY and the colours, not to where any element of the picture sits.
THE PICTURE AS A WHOLE IS ANCHORED. It does not rotate, spin, swing, sweep sideways or slide across the room as one sheet. Its outer boundary stays where it is. The motion lives INSIDE it, element by element:
- THE CHIMNEY SMOKE - every single chimney in the village is smoking, not one of them without its plume. Each thin curl climbs upward out of its chimney pot, leaning slightly to the right, rising about one roof height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the ribbons, while a fresh curl forms at the pot behind it. The plumes are never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the village chimneys and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the chimneys continuously so the column never empties.
- THE RIBBONS - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon radiates outward from the great star and ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running down the band away from the star, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall fan stays where it is on the plaster; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the ceiling is doing this at the same time.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling on a long sweeping curve away from where it sits in the start image, arcing in toward the great star, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 5.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes.
- THE WOODLAND DEER AND THE ELVES. The illustrated deer travel steadily among the projected fir trees along the lower wall, legs walking and heads nodding, moving about one body length every second. Existing rabbits make small grounded hops within the wall drawing; elves bend, lift existing wrapped gifts and pass them toward the red sleigh at the log-cabin depot. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.
- THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.
- THE ELVES AND THE FLOATING THINGS - the tiny elves keep passing gifts outside the log-cabin gift depot, and the floating baubles and candy canes drift through the scene at their own depths, each one turning slowly as it goes, while the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with the clappers moving inside them.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - each one drifts downward and slightly sideways about a quarter of the wall's height every second, the way snow falls past a window at night. Each turns slowly on its own centre as it drifts and breathes brighter and dimmer on its own timing. One that reaches the skirting board fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the seams of the ceiling, so the density holds even. Track this one so the fall is unmistakable: a large cream six-point flake that begins just above the headboard, falls steadily and drifts slightly right, passes the middle of the wall at 2.5s and fades out low near the skirting at 5.0s.
- THE SHOOTING STARS - the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade; fresh ones set off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE GREAT CHRISTMAS STAR - the gold eight-pointed star is the ONLY element that holds its centre position. It stays exactly where it is on the ceiling for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.
Because the wall and the whole ceiling are lit by the SAME projector, they are one picture and behave identically at the same instant. Whatever the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. There is never a still ceiling above a moving wall. Nothing is painted, printed or stencilled on the plaster.

TIMELINE - THE CAMERA MAKES EXACTLY TWO MOVES AND NO MORE, and they are the opposite of the other clips in this set: this one starts high and comes DOWN.
0.0-2.2s the camera is aimed HIGH at the start, weighted to the top of the cream plaster wall and the pitched ceiling with tie beams so the projected picture fills most of the frame and THE BOY is small and low near the bottom edge. From 0.0s the camera TILTS DOWN in one slow steady continuous move, the ribbons rippling, the travelling gold light waves spreading and the sleigh flying across the pitched ceiling with tie beams as it goes, until the bed and THE BOY fill the lower half. THE BOY bends his knees and pushes off the mattress; both feet leave the quilt, arms swinging up over his head, mouth open laughing. The mattress compresses and springs back.
2.2-5.0s the tilt ends and the camera PULLS BACK a little in one slow steady move, the frame opening slightly wider so a little more of the bedroom comes in at the edges - a small gentle zoom out, nothing sudden. THE BOY lands on both feet and drives straight into a second, higher jump, knees tucking, then a third; he never stops moving and the body motion carries straight across the seam between the two camera moves.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed - a real boy jumping at real speed and landing with real weight. Exactly one child. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes exactly two moves: a slow tilt DOWN, then a small gentle pull back. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Wall and ceiling always move together. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a camera that tilts UP; a camera that pushes in; more than two camera moves; a hard or fast zoom; a frozen or still pattern; chimneys that are not smoking; an ribbons that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a deer procession that does not travel or elves that do not move; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above a moving wall; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; the projector unit in shot; a second child; an adult; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the deer procession advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - The two roof slopes, straight central ridge and three white-painted tie beams remain clearly legible. The sleigh and garlands fold at the ridge and stretch down both roof planes. Each tie beam catches its own slice of the projected picture on the face toward the source, with a clear offset to the plaster beyond and a narrow hard shadow behind it. At the triangular gable and window casings the picture breaks again along the actual wall-to-ceiling and trim edges. All physical walls, ceiling planes, beams, seams and trim remain stationary.
```

ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa and eight reindeer sweep past a gold Christmas star, with curling red-and-gold ribbon trails interwoven with holly leaves, candy canes, delicate cream snowflakes and tiny star lights. This is unmistakably Christmas Eve, red and gold dominant with deep fir-green accents, warm windows and a dark midnight background. Each thin curl climbs upward out of its chimney pot, leaning slightly to the right, rising about one roof height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the ribbons, while a fresh curl forms at the pot behind it. The plumes are never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the village chimneys and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the chimneys continuously so the column never empties.
- THE RIBBONS - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon radiates outward from the great star and ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running down the band away from the star, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall fan stays where it is on the plaster; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the ceiling is doing this at the same time.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling on a long sweeping curve away from where it sits in the start image, arcing in toward the great star, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 5.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes.
- THE WOODLAND DEER AND THE ELVES. The illustrated deer travel steadily among the projected fir trees along the lower wall, legs walking and heads nodding, moving about one body length every second. Existing rabbits make small grounded hops within the wall drawing; elves bend, lift existing wrapped gifts and pass them toward the red sleigh at the log-cabin depot. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.
- THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.
- THE ELVES AND THE FLOATING THINGS - the tiny elves keep passing gifts outside the log-cabin gift depot, and the floating baubles and candy canes drift through the scene at their own depths, each one turning slowly as it goes, while the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with the clappers moving inside them.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - each one drifts downward and slightly sideways about a quarter of the wall's height every second, the way snow falls past a window at night. Each turns slowly on its own centre as it drifts and breathes brighter and dimmer on its own timing. One that reaches the skirting board fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the seams of the ceiling, so the density holds even. Track this one so the fall is unmistakable: a large cream six-point flake that begins just above the headboard, falls steadily and drifts slightly right, passes the middle of the wall at 2.5s and fades out low near the skirting at 5.0s.
- THE SHOOTING STARS - the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade; fresh ones set off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE GREAT CHRISTMAS STAR - the gold eight-pointed star is the ONLY element that holds its centre position. It stays exactly where it is on the ceiling for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.

THE BOY lands on both feet and drives straight into a second, higher jump, knees tucking, then a third; he never stops moving and the body motion carries straight across the seam between the two camera moves.

A red sleigh filled with colourful ribboned parcels waits outside Santa's warm log-cabin gift depot, with red-coated elves carrying teddy bears and wooden rocking horses toward it. Along the upper walls, paired gold bells, holly clusters and richly woven evergreen swags. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa and eight reindeer sweep past a gold Christmas star, with curling red-and-gold ribbon trails interwoven with holly leaves, candy canes, delicate cream snowflakes and tiny star lights. This is unmistakably Christmas Eve, red and gold dominant with deep fir-green accents, warm windows and a dark midnight background. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

HOW THE PICTURE BEHAVES - THIS IS THE ENTIRE POINT OF THE CLIP. In every previous attempt the picture sat dead still like printed wallpaper and the clip was worthless. THE PICTURE IS A FILM PLAYING ON A CINEMA SCREEN: the screen is the plaster and holds perfectly still, while everything inside the picture is in constant motion for all five seconds. - THE RISING SPARK DRAWINGS - flat gold pixels climb along the wall from the illustrated village chimneys toward the painted star field at roughly one roof height each half-second. They are part of the projected wall movie, with no real sparks, airborne trails or particles between the camera and the wall.
- THE RIBBONS - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon radiates outward from the great star and ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running down the band away from the star, about half of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall fan stays where it is on the plaster; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the ceiling is doing this at the same time.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling on a long sweeping curve away from where it sits in the start image, arcing in toward the great star, covering about a quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so by 5.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes.
- THE WOODLAND DEER AND THE ELVES. The illustrated deer travel steadily among the projected fir trees along the lower wall, legs walking and heads nodding, moving about two body lengths every second. Existing rabbits make small grounded hops within the wall drawing; elves bend, lift existing wrapped gifts and pass them toward the red sleigh at the log-cabin depot. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.
- THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about half of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.
- THE ELVES AND THE PROJECTED ORNAMENT DRAWINGS - the tiny elves keep passing gifts within the flat wall illustration beside the log-cabin depot. Flat bauble and candy-cane drawings translate and turn along the wall and ceiling planes; they have no depth in the real room. Big gold bell drawings swing visibly side to side within the same projected surface, their clapper drawings moving inside them. All obey surface perspective and architectural occlusion.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - these are strictly flat two-dimensional moving light marks on plaster, never physical flakes. On the ceiling they glide tangentially ALONG each roof plane toward the ridge or wall seam, following its perspective; they never fall away from the ceiling into the room. On the walls they translate downward along the wall at about one quarter wall-height per second. At a wall-ceiling corner their path bends onto the adjoining surface and remains flush with it; behind the real boy, bed, headboard and beams they are occluded and disappear, never drawn over those objects. Keep their projected scale tied to the surface, with no foreground enlargement, blur, depth or free-space parallax. Every flake stays visibly attached to one architectural plane in every frame.
- THE SHOOTING STARS - the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade; fresh ones set off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE GREAT CHRISTMAS STAR - the gold eight-pointed star is the ONLY element that holds its centre position. It stays exactly where it is on the ceiling for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.
Because the wall and the whole ceiling are lit by the SAME projector, they are one picture and behave identically at the same instant. Whatever the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. There is never a still ceiling above a moving wall. Nothing is painted, printed or stencilled on the plaster.

TIMELINE - THE CAMERA MAKES EXACTLY TWO MOVES AND NO MORE, and they are the opposite of the other clips in this set: this one starts high and comes DOWN.
0.0-2.2s the camera is aimed HIGH at the start, weighted to the top of the cream plaster wall and the pitched ceiling with tie beams so the projected picture fills most of the frame and THE BOY is small and low near the bottom edge. From 0.0s the camera TILTS DOWN in one slow steady continuous move, the ribbons rippling, the travelling gold light waves spreading and the sleigh flying across the pitched ceiling with tie beams as it goes, until the bed and THE BOY fill the lower half. THE BOY bends his knees and pushes off the mattress; both feet leave the quilt, arms swinging up over his head, mouth open laughing. The mattress compresses and springs back.
2.2-5.0s the tilt ends and the camera PULLS BACK a little in one slow steady move, the frame opening slightly wider so a little more of the bedroom comes in at the edges - a small gentle zoom out, nothing sudden. THE BOY lands on both feet and drives straight into a second, higher jump, knees tucking, then a third; he never stops moving and the body motion carries straight across the seam between the two camera moves.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed - a real boy jumping at real speed and landing with real weight. Exactly one child. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes exactly two moves: a slow tilt DOWN, then a small gentle pull back. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Wall and ceiling always move together. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the deer procession advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - The two roof slopes, straight central ridge and three white-painted tie beams remain clearly legible. The sleigh and garlands fold at the ridge and stretch down both roof planes.
