```text
SUBJECTS:
THE SMALLER CHILD - a boy of about five with East Asian features, light warm skin and straight dark hair in a neat side part, on the rug in front of the sofa, back to camera and slightly to the side, head tipped back looking up at the projected ceiling with his arms out in wonder, turning slowly on the spot.
THE OTHER CHILD - a girl of about eight with East Asian features, light warm skin and long straight dark hair with a red velvet hairband, jumping on the sofa cushions, caught mid-air above them with her knees bent and her arms flung up, the cushion compressed under where she pushed off, bouncing on the spot.
Both in warm red and ivory knitted Christmas jumpers with gold bells and green fir-tree patterns, navy knitted lounge trousers and cream wool socks. Both stay small in frame, in scale with the huge room. Exactly two children. No third child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running in the room, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the back wall and the whole coved ceiling. Everything glowing on the plaster, wall material and ceiling details is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical WOODLAND CHRISTMAS GIFT NIGHT: a snowy forest of enormous decorated fir trees, every branch hung with crimson baubles, gold candle lights and tiny ribboned gifts; nestled between the trees, warmly lit log cottages decorated with holly wreaths and red bows; friendly illustrated deer with gold bells and rabbits beside little stacks of wrapped Christmas presents. A red sleigh filled with colourful ribboned parcels waits outside Santa's warm log-cabin gift depot, with red-coated elves carrying teddy bears and wooden rocking horses toward it. Two monumental gold Christmas lanterns flank a towering fully decorated tree; every ornament has a crisp rim and every gift ribbon a visible knot. Along the upper walls, paired gold bells, holly clusters and richly woven evergreen swags. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa and eight reindeer sweep past a gold Christmas star, with curling red-and-gold ribbon trails interwoven with holly leaves, candy canes, delicate cream snowflakes and tiny star lights. This is unmistakably Christmas Eve, red and gold dominant with deep fir-green accents, warm windows and a dark midnight background. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

HOW THE PICTURE BEHAVES - THIS IS THE ENTIRE POINT OF THE CLIP. In every previous attempt the picture sat dead still like printed wallpaper and the clip was worthless. THE PICTURE IS A FILM PLAYING ON A CINEMA SCREEN: the screen is the plaster, wall material and ceiling details and holds perfectly still, while everything inside the picture is in constant motion for all four seconds. The start image shows the film already fully projected across the walls AND the entire ceiling. From the first frame continue the moving artwork at the same full coverage and brightness. Start motion within the existing picture, never the appearance of the picture itself. Matching @Image 1 applies to the great room, the furniture, the children and the colours, not to where any element of the picture sits.
THE PICTURE AS A WHOLE IS ANCHORED. It does not rotate, spin, swing, sweep sideways or slide across the room as one sheet. Its outer boundary stays where it is. The motion lives INSIDE it, element by element:
- THE CHIMNEY SMOKE - every single chimney in the village is smoking, not one of them without its plume. Each thin curl climbs upward out of its chimney pot, leaning slightly to the right, rising about one roof height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the ribbons, while a fresh curl forms at the pot behind it. The plumes are never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the village chimneys and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the chimneys continuously so the column never empties.
- THE RIBBONS - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon radiates outward from the great star and ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running down the band away from the star, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall fan stays where it is on the plaster; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the wall and on the ceiling is doing this at the same time.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling on a long sweeping curve away from where it sits in the start image, arcing in toward the great star, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 4.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes. The team is never parked.
- THE WOODLAND DEER AND THE ELVES. The illustrated deer travel steadily among the projected fir trees along the lower wall, legs walking and heads nodding, moving about one body length every second. Existing rabbits make small grounded hops within the wall drawing; elves bend, lift existing wrapped gifts and pass them toward the red sleigh at the log-cabin depot. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.
- THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - each one drifts downward and slightly sideways about a quarter of the wall's height every second, the way snow falls past a window at night. Each turns slowly on its own centre as it drifts and breathes brighter and dimmer on its own timing. One that reaches the floor line fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the plaster ceiling details, so the density holds even.
- THE SMALL TRAVELLERS - the red-and-white striped candy canes and the glowing baubles turn slowly end over end as they drift; the tiny elves keep passing gifts beside the log-cabin gift depot and the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with the clappers moving inside them; the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade, with fresh ones setting off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE GREAT CHRISTMAS STAR - the gold eight-pointed star is the ONLY element that holds its centre position. It stays exactly where it is on the ceiling for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.
Because the back wall and the whole ceiling are lit by the SAME projector, they are one picture and behave identically at the same instant. Whatever the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. There is never a still ceiling above a moving wall. Nothing is painted, printed or stencilled on the plaster, wall material and ceiling details. NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR: the pale oak floorboards, the cream wool rug, the oatmeal linen sectional sofa, the long ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry no projected shapes at all.

TIMELINE:
0.0-1.0s the person filming takes one clear step forward; the frame moves in and settles. THE OTHER CHILD bounces on the sofa cushions with both arms up, laughing. THE SMALLER CHILD turns on the rug with his face tipped up.
1.0-2.0s the camera holds in the closer position. THE OTHER CHILD pushes up harder and drops back down onto the cushion, her knees bending and springing back. THE SMALLER CHILD keeps turning and tips his head further back.
2.0-4.0s the camera tilts up in one slow smooth move past the top of the tree until the coved ceiling fills the upper half of the frame, the ribbons rippling along it, the travelling gold light waves still spreading outward across it from the great star and the sleigh team still flying in toward that star. Both children stay low in the frame and keep moving.

AUDIO: room tone, two children laughing and squealing, feet thumping on the sofa cushions, feet on a rug. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed throughout - real children moving at real speed with real weight. Exactly two children, and their knitted jumpers read clearly. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes one move per beat and no more. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Walls and ceiling always move together. THE OTHER CHILD stays on the sofa. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

DO NOT INCLUDE: blank or partly blank walls or ceiling at the start; a projection appearing, switching on, fading in, spreading, growing or revealing itself during the shot; an opening before-and-after transformation; a frozen or still pattern; chimneys that are not smoking; an ribbons that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a deer procession that does not travel or elves that do not move; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above moving walls; an un-animated area of the coved ceiling or an un-animated ceiling detail in any frame; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; children standing still; a third child; an adult; the projector unit in shot; jellyfish, fish, coral or bubbles; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the deer procession advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - The broad flat central ceiling and all four smooth coved transitions remain visible. The sleigh trail and garlands run across the flat panel then curve and progressively stretch around the rounded coves before continuing vertically down the walls; no invented ridge or angular fold interrupts the coves. At the chimney breast the picture breaks sharply around the two projecting vertical corners, carrying across every brick face with crisp mortar-line texture and tiny mortar shadows. The arched doorway and window reveals interrupt the light at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All physical walls, ceiling planes, beams, seams and trim remain stationary.
```

```text
REFERENCE ROLES: the FIRST attached image owns the geometry, camera position, framing, pose and prop placement. THE PEOPLE - REMOVE AND REPLACE: the mother who is up on the staircase in the reference is replaced by a different MOTHER in exactly her position, at exactly her height on the stairs, at exactly her distance from the camera and exactly her size in the frame - she is up on the upper run of the staircase behind the slim black wrought-iron railing, leaning far out over that railing toward the Christmas tree, one hand gripping the rail, her other arm stretched out across the gap toward the top of the tree, holding a Christmas tree topper and reaching to set it on the highest tip. She is a mother in her mid 30s with golden-blonde hair in a low ponytail, fair skin and a softly angular face. She is on the STAIRS, over the railing - not on the floor. Below her, standing together on the hall FLOOR at the bottom of the frame over to the LEFT side of the frame, on the opposite side of the picture from the staircase and the newel post, well clear of the tree and NOT in front of it - the floor directly in front of the tree's base is left completely empty - with their backs to the camera, small in the frame because they are far below the top of the tree, two children - a boy about 9 with short curly light-brown hair and a girl about 4 with dark auburn hair in two little pigtails - are seen from behind, standing on the floor with their heads tipped back looking up at their mother and at the top of the tall tree, the boy holding the little girl's hand. The tree still towers over all of them and still fills the height of the frame exactly as it does in the reference.

THEIR CLOTHES: all three wear matching American-flag Christmas pyjamas - pyjama tops with bold red and white horizontal stripes and a navy blue yoke across the shoulders covered in small white stars, and matching red and white striped pyjama bottoms, soft brushed cotton, slightly loose the way real pyjamas fit - with thick cream wool socks.

The tree stays exactly as it is in the reference: the same very tall slim green artificial tree, the same dense old-fashioned dressing of mismatched multicoloured glass baubles, candy canes, snowmen, tinsel garland and warm white string lights, and the same wrapped presents piled at its base. THE VERY TOP OF THE TREE IS BARE - there is no star and no topper sitting on it, because the mother is in the act of reaching out from the stairs to place one there; the gold star topper is in her outstretched hand, held just clear of the highest tip and not yet fixed to it.

Do not pull the camera back or shrink the tree to make that room.

COLOUR: neutral white balance, the way an iPhone auto-corrects a warm room. The picture is NOT orange, NOT amber, NOT sepia and there is no warm colour cast laid over the whole frame. The walls read as clean pale blue-grey paint, the staircase and floor as natural honey-oak wood, the flag keeps true red, white and navy.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: bright, clean and evenly lit daytime interior, plenty of light on the walls and on the family, no dark corners, no heavy shadows. Shot on an iPhone and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere, crisp detail in the ornaments, the tinsel and the knit of the pyjamas, realistic skin and fabric texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

No captions, no watermarks, no lettering, no user-interface elements and no social-media overlays anywhere in the image.
```

#### Video prompt
```text
Single continuous handheld iPhone shot, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, filmed by someone standing on the hallway floor holding the phone up — real held-phone look with natural handheld sway, no cuts, no zoom, no lens push-in, framing locked exactly as in the start frame for the whole clip.

Start frame: a woman in American-flag pajamas stands on the staircase behind the honey-oak wooden railing, leaning out over it with one arm stretched up to the gold star on top of the tall decorated Christmas tree beside the stairs. Two small children in matching American-flag pajamas stand on the floor at the lower left of the frame with their backs to the camera, watching her. Wrapped presents at the foot of the tree.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0–1.0s — Both hands now on the gold star, she works it down onto the top of the tree, stretching further and further out over the railing, more and more of her weight going onto the tree itself.
1.0–1.6s — The tree tips. It leans away from the stairs toward the open floor and keeps going, and she loses her balance and comes over the railing with it, still holding on. At the same instant the two children take three quick steps further to the left, well out of the way, and stop there — the tree never comes near them.
1.6–2.4s — The whole tree swings down diagonally across the frame and crashes flat onto the wooden floor, branches flattening, the gold star snapping off the top and skittering away on its own.
2.4–3.2s — She lands on the floor face-down across the fallen tree, and the impact bursts a wave of ornaments off the branches — dozens of red, gold, pink and white baubles bouncing out low across the wooden floor and rolling toward the camera, spreading right across the bottom of the frame.
3.2–5.0s — The last ornaments wobble to a stop on the floor. She lies still across the fallen tree for a beat, then lifts her head, blows the hair out of her face and starts laughing. The two children stay where they moved to on the left, staring at the mess. End frame: the tree flat on the floor, ornaments scattered everywhere, her looking up laughing.

AUDIO: her real voice, lip-synced, matching her appearance — a short yelp as she goes over, then breathless laughing at the end; the dry rush of the tree branches going over; the heavy thud of the tree hitting the wooden floor; dozens of glass and plastic ornaments clattering, bouncing and rolling on hard wood; one small surprised squeal from a child. (no BGM). No subtitles.

REALISM — HARD RULES: she keeps exactly the same face, hair and American-flag pajamas as the start frame the whole clip, and the two children keep exactly the same clothes and size. The hallway stays pixel-faithful to the start frame — same staircase, same honey-oak wooden railing and newel post, same American flag on the wall, same wooden floor, same daylight. The children are never touched by the tree and are never underneath it. Real weight and gravity — the tree topples on its own mass and stops on the floor, she drops straight down, nobody is thrown or launched. Real phone-camera look, slight sensor noise, mild motion blur on the fall, no cinematic bokeh, no slow motion, no text overlays, no watermark. No subtitles, no captions and no on-screen words of any kind anywhere in the picture.

POSITIVE LOCKS: everything plays at real-time speed. One continuous take from 0.0s to 5.0s. The camera never moves from where it is in the start frame.
```

AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The two roof slopes, straight central ridge and three white-painted tie beams remain clearly legible. The sleigh and garlands fold at the ridge and stretch down both roof planes. Each tie beam catches its own slice of the projected picture on the face toward the source, with a clear offset to the plaster beyond and a narrow hard shadow behind it. At the triangular gable and window casings the picture breaks again along the actual wall-to-ceiling and trim edges. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes one move per beat and no more. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Walls and ceiling always move together. THE OTHER CHILD stays on the sofa. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

````text
DO NOT INCLUDE: blank or partly blank walls or ceiling at the start; a projection appearing, switching on, fading in, spreading, growing or revealing itself during the shot; an opening before-and-after transformation; a frozen or still pattern; chimneys that are not smoking; an ribbons that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a deer procession that does not travel or elves that do not move; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above moving walls; an un-animated area of the coved ceiling or an un-animated ceiling detail in any frame; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; children standing still; a third child; an adult; the projector unit in shot; jellyfish, fish, coral or bubbles; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the deer procession advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - The broad flat central ceiling and all four smooth coved transitions remain visible. The sleigh trail and garlands run across the flat panel then curve and progressively stretch around the rounded coves before continuing vertically down the walls; no invented ridge or angular fold interrupts the coves. At the chimney breast the picture breaks sharply around the two projecting vertical corners, carrying across every brick face with crisp mortar-line texture and tiny mortar shadows. The arched doorway and window reveals interrupt the light at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All physical walls, ceiling planes, beams, seams and trim remain stationary.
````
